This leads to an im<sub>p</sub>ortant <sub>q</sub>uestion: (RQ1) Can explainability-aware noise calibration during FL training improve both predictive performance and explanation fidelity while preserving formal DP guarantees?

Be<sub>y</sub>ond the established <sub>p</sub>rivac<sub>y</sub>-utilit<sub>y p</sub>aradi<sub>g</sub>m<sub>,</sub> recent work <sup>d</sup>emonstrates t<sup>h</sup>at mu<sup>l</sup>tip<sup>l</sup>e mo<sup>d</sup>e<sup>l</sup> o<sup>b</sup>jectives interact non-trivia<sup>ll</sup>y under DP [20]. For exam<sub>p</sub>le, techni<sub>q</sub>ues desi<sub>g</sub>ned to im<sub>p</sub>rove <sub>p</sub>redictive <sub>p</sub>erformance under strict <sub>p</sub>rivac<sub>y</sub> bud<sub>g</sub>ets can inadvertentl<sub>y</sub> am<sub>p</sub>lif<sub>y</sub> model bias<sub>,</sub> revealin<sub>g</sub> a com<sub>p</sub>lex tension between <sub>p</sub>rivac<sub>y,</sub> utilit<sub>y</sub>, and fairness [22]. This leads to two additional open questions. First, (RQ2) can adaptive DP noise calibration translate cumulative privacy loss into larger gains in accuracy and explanation fidelity than static noise allocation? After com<sub>p</sub>letin<sub>g</sub> its local trainin<sub>g</sub> ste<sub>p</sub>s<sub>,</sub> the resultin<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>arameters form client �’s u<sub>p</sub>dated <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>,</sub> d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as</sub> $\mathbf { w } _ { i } ^ { ( t + 1 ) }$ <sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e server</sub> th<sub>en aggrega</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>ese mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o</sub> f<sub>orm</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>new</sub> <sub>g</sub>l<sub>o</sub>b<sub>a</sub>l <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>:</sub>

To <sub>p</sub>rovide formal <sub>p</sub>rivac<sub>y</sub> <sub>g</sub>uarantees<sub>,</sub> FL is commonl<sub>y</sub> combined with Diferential Privac<sub>y</sub> (DP), which bounds the information that can be inferred about an<sub>y</sub> sin<sub>g</sub>le individual from the trainin<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>rocess. A randomized mechanism M is $( \varepsilon , \delta ) \mathrm { - d i f f e r e n t i a l l y }$ <sub>p</sub>ri<sub>v</sub>ate if<sub>,</sub> for an<sub>y</sub> nei<sub>g</sub>hborin<sub>g</sub> datasets $D$ <sub>an</sub>d $D ^ { \prime }$ dif<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>n</sub> <sub>one</sub> <sub>recor</sub>d<sub>,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sup>for</sup> <sup>an</sup>y <sup>out</sup>p<sup>ut</sup> <sup>set</sup> ${ \bar { \boldsymbol { s } } } ,$

## 4.1 Main Idea

We extend the standard FL trainin<sub>g</sub> (see server-side Al<sub>g</sub>orithm 2 in A<sub>pp</sub>endix A) with an ex<sub>p</sub>lainabilit<sub>y</sub>-driven client trainin<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>rocedure (Al<sub>g</sub>orithm 1) that im<sub>p</sub>roves the faithfulness of model ex<sub>p</sub>lanations while <sub>p</sub>reservin<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>redictive <sub>p</sub>erformance and <sub>p</sub>rovidin<sub>g</sub> DP (Fi<sub>g</sub> ure 1). The key idea is to adapt the amount of DP noise injected into <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>m</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>-</sub>b<sub>a</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h <sub>up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> b<sub>ase</sub>d <sub>on</sub> <sub>an</sub> <sub>exp</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>na</sub>bilit<sub>y</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l <sub>re</sub>fl<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> the model’s <sub>p</sub>redictions. // Compose the explainability signal:   
12 $s _ { \mathrm { r a w } } \gets \left( \alpha \cdot \Delta \ell + \beta \cdot m _ { \mathrm { c f } } \right) c ^ { \gamma } ;$   
13 $s _ { \mathrm { r a w } } \gets \mathrm { C } _ { \mathrm { L I P } } ( s _ { \mathrm { r a w } } , 0 , 1 ) ;$   
// Privatize the explainability signal:   
14 $\tilde { s } _ { \mathrm { r a w } } \gets \mathrm { C L I P } \big ( s _ { \mathrm { r a w } } + N ( 0 , \sigma _ { s } ^ { 2 } ) , 0 , 1 \big ) ;$   
// Smooth the explainability signal:   
15 $s \gets ( 1 - \tau ) s + \tau \tilde { s } _ { \mathrm { r a w } } ;$   
// Adjust DP noise based on Xpl. signal:   
16 $\sigma _ { \mathrm { s t e p } } \gets \sigma _ { \mathrm { m a x } } - s ( \sigma _ { \mathrm { m a x } } - \sigma _ { \mathrm { m i n } } ) ;$   
// Privacy filter:   
17 $\sigma _ { \mathrm { s t e p } } $ <sub>sma</sub>ll<sub>es</sub>t <sub>a</sub>d<sub>m</sub>i<sub>ss</sub>ibl<sub>e</sub> $\sigma \in [ \sigma _ { \mathrm { s t e p } } , \sigma _ { \mathrm { m a x } } ] ;$   
18 if no such � exists then   
19 return $( \mathbf { w } _ { i } ^ { t } )$ ; // Budget exhausted   
20 end   
// DP Accounting:   
21 Char<sub>g</sub>e the accountant with a ste<sub>p</sub> at $\sigma _ { \mathrm { { s t e p } } } ;$   
// DP-SGD update for this mini-batch:   
22 $g _ { k } \gets \nabla _ { \mathrm w } \mathcal { L } ( \mathbf w _ { i } ^ { t } ; x _ { k } , y _ { k } )$ f<sub>or</sub> $k = 1 , \ldots , B ;$   
23 $g _ { k } \gets \frac { g _ { k } } { \operatorname* { m a x } \bigl ( 1 , \ : \| g _ { k } \| _ { 2 } / C \bigr ) }$ f<sub>or a</sub>ll � <sub>;</sub> // Clipping to �   
24 $\begin{array} { r } { \tilde { g } \gets \frac { 1 } { B } \Big ( \sum _ { k = 1 } ^ { B } g _ { k } + N ( 0 , \sigma _ { \mathrm { s t e p } } ^ { 2 } C ^ { 2 } \mathbf { I } ) \Big ) ; } \end{array}$   
25 $\mathbf { w } _ { i } ^ { t }  \dot { \mathbf { w } } _ { i } ^ { t } - \eta \tilde { g } ;$   
26 U<sub>p</sub>date cumulative <sub>p</sub>rivac<sub>y</sub> loss $\varepsilon _ { \mathrm { c u m } , i } ;$   
27 end   
28 return $\mathbf { w } _ { i } ^ { ( t ) }$

## 9 Discussion and Limitations

Thi<sub>s wo</sub>rk intr<sub>o</sub>d<sub>uce</sub>d XC<sub>a</sub>l-FL<sub>, a</sub>n <sub>a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> n<sub>o</sub>i<sub>se co</sub>ntr<sub>o</sub>l m<sub>ec</sub>h-<sub>a</sub>ni<sub>s</sub>m f<sub>o</sub>r DP-FL th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>ca</sub>libr<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> n<sub>o</sub>i<sub>se</sub> <sub>us</sub>in<sub>g</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>co</sub>m<sub>pos</sub>it<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>g</sub>n<sub>a</sub>l combinin<sub>g</sub> ex<sub>p</sub>lainabilit<sub>y</sub>- and decision-related measures. The three si<sub>g</sub>nal com<sub>p</sub>onents ofer com<sub>p</sub>lementar<sub>y</sub> contributions: the lo<sub>g</sub>it chan<sub>g</sub>e <sub>p</sub>reserves learnin<sub>g</sub> from informative salient re<sub>g</sub>ions<sub>,</sub> the <sub>coun</sub>t<sub>er</sub>f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>marg</sub>i<sub>n s</sub>h<sub>arpens</sub> d<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>i<sub>es, an</sub>d th<sub>e sa</sub>li<sub>ency</sub> concentration favors com<sub>p</sub>act attention <sub>p</sub>atterns. To<sub>g</sub>ether<sub>,</sub> these <sub>componen</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>ena</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>ner-gra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>no</sub>i<sub>se</sub> <sub>ca</sub>lib<sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> th<sub>an</sub> <sub>any</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e</sub> com<sub>p</sub>onent alone, as confirmed b<sub>y</sub> our ablation stud<sub>y</sub> (Section 7), <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>a</sub>l<sub>ance</sub>d <sub>con</sub>fi<sub>gura</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> $( \alpha = \beta = \gamma = 1 )$ ) consistentl<sub>y</sub> <sub>ac</sub>hi<sub>eve</sub>d th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>ronges</sub>t <sub>or mos</sub>t <sub>ro</sub>b<sub>us</sub>t <sub>resu</sub>lt<sub>s across</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t<sub>s an</sub>d <sub>p</sub>rivac<sub>y</sub> levels. This a<sub>pp</sub>roach is motivated b<sub>y</sub> <sub>p</sub>rior findin<sub>g</sub>s that DP mechanisms with similar nominal <sub>p</sub>rivac<sub>y g</sub>uarantees can <sub>y</sub>ield diferent downstream behavior [15, 27], and extends recent work on ex<sub>p</sub>lainabilit<sub>y</sub>-aware ada<sub>p</sub>tive noise allocation in FL [30].

## F Detailed Complexity Analysis

We <sub>p</sub>erformed an execution runtime com<sub>p</sub>lexit<sub>y</sub> anal<sub>y</sub>sis with res<sub>p</sub>ect to our used hardware (a machine with a 64GB of RAM and a NVIDIA’s RTX 2080 Ti GPU) across all three datasets and our t<sub>wo</sub> <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ures.</sub> S<sub>pec</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y,</sub> <sub>we</sub> <sub>measure</sub>d b<sub>o</sub>th th<sub>e wa</sub>ll<sub>-c</sub>l<sub>oc</sub>k d<sub>ura</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f <sub>a s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e c</sub>li<sub>en</sub>t t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng roun</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>ncurre</sub>d <sub>over</sub>h<sub>ea</sub>d <sub>re</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>d <sub>c</sub>li<sub>en</sub>t<sub>-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l DP<sub>-</sub>SGD trainin<sub>g</sub> (with static DP noise).

A standard DP-SGD trainin<sub>g</sub> ste<sub>p</sub> costs a<sub>pp</sub>roximatel<sub>y</sub> $3 { \cal F } + { \cal O } ( { \cal P } )$ <sub>p</sub>er mini-batch<sub>,</sub> where � is the cost of a sin<sub>g</sub>le trainin<sub>g</sub>-mode for-<sub>war</sub>d <sub>pass</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>an</sub>d � i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>o</sub>t<sub>a</sub>l <sub>num</sub>b<sub>er o</sub>f <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>parame</sub>t<sub>ers:</sub> <sub>one</sub> f<sub>orwar</sub>d <sub>pass</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>t<sub>es</sub> � <sub>,</sub> <sub>one</sub> b<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>war</sub>d <sub>pass</sub> contributes ≈ 2� , and batch-level <sub>g</sub>radient cli<sub>pp</sub>in<sub>g</sub> with noise injection adds O(�) [1, 24]. XCal-FL adds an ex<sub>p</sub>lainabilit<sub>y</sub>-driven si<sub>g</sub>nal com<sub>p</sub>utation <sub>p</sub>er mini-batch<sub>,</sub> consistin<sub>g</sub> of two inferencemode forward <sub>p</sub>asses (one on the ori<sub>g</sub>inal in<sub>p</sub>ut and one on the masked in<sub>p</sub>ut), followed b<sub>y</sub> salienc<sub>y</sub> mask o<sub>p</sub>erations on the last <sub>convo</sub>l<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> <sub>map.</sub> A<sub>n</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence-mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub> f<sub>orwar</sub>d <sub>pass,</sub> $F _ { \mathrm { i n f : } }$ <sup>costs a</sup>pp<sup>roximatel</sup>y $0 . 5 – 0 . 7 \times F$ since no com<sub>p</sub>utation <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h is constr<sub>u</sub>cted and no intermediate acti<sub>v</sub>ations are stored. The <sub>p</sub>erste<sub>p</sub> cost of XCal-FL is therefore $3 F + 2 \cdot F _ { \mathrm { i n f } } + O ( P )$ <sub>.</sub> N<sub>o</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>y, no</sub> <sub>a</sub>dditi<sub>ona</sub>l b<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>war</sub>d <sub>pass</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>requ</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>exp</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>na</sub>bilit<sub>y</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l<sub>.</sub> Thi<sub>s y</sub>i<sub>e</sub>ld<sub>s a</sub> th<sub>eore</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>over</sub>h<sub>ea</sub>d <sub>o</sub> $\mathrm { f } 2 \cdot F _ { \mathrm { i n f } } / ( 3 F + O ( P ) )$ .
